Đây là hướng tôi khuyên tiếp tục. Không đổi kiến trúc nữa, tránh phát sinh thêm các nhánh thiết kế khiến dự án kéo dài không cần thiết.


* done
Beginning with the audit of the pieces I'll extend, then implementing the activation layer.































## Verdict: **APPROVED WITH EXPLICIT CONDITIONS**

Device identities are now generated by libsignal itself, sealed on the device, and **independently proven to the server**. No device is marked verified, and nothing is encrypted yet.

### What was built

**`core/security/DeviceIdentityManager.kt`** — generates identity, signed prekey (signed by the identity key), Kyber-1024 prekey, 50 one-time prekeys and a registration id, then writes one envelope. Every mutator is `@Synchronized`, so two concurrent activations cannot create two identities. `Lost` **throws** rather than silently replacing an identity the server still trusts.

**Storage v2** — envelope gains signed/Kyber/one-time record slots with `-1` null markers; `decode` accepts **v1 and v2**, so an existing identity is read and upgraded in place, never discarded. All guarantees unchanged: Keystore AES-GCM, AAD bound to the device key, atomic rename, `noBackupFilesDir`, redacted `toString()`.

**Proof of possession** — the part that makes the rest trustworthy:

1. `POST …/identity-challenge` → 32 random bytes, 5-minute TTL, bound to the device row.
2. Client signs `"KONNEX-POP-V1" 0x00 rowId 0x00 identityKey 0x00 challenge`.
3. `POST …/identity-proof` burns the challenge, verifies with libsignal's `ECPublicKey.verifySignature`, and only then sets `proof_verified = 1`.

The server needed `libsignal-client:0.86.5` because XEdDSA cannot be checked by a standard Ed25519 library. Only the endpoint writes the flag — no client-supplied boolean is trusted, and `putDeviceKeys` resets it to 0 on any key-version bump, so an identity change always forces re-verification.

### Evidence

- **Server: 10 suites, 66 tests, 0 failures** (was 60; +6 proof tests).
- `DeviceIdentityProofTest`: valid proof sets the flag · wrong key → 403 · replay → 410 · another device's row → 403 · revoked device → 401 · unknown challenge → 410.
- `core:security` JVM: 11 tests green (including v2 round-trip and v1 compatibility).
- `:app:assembleDebug` with libsignal linked and desugaring on: **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**.

### Gaps I will not paper over

- **Device verification is BLOCKED as a user-facing feature.** `sasFor()` works and returns libsignal's canonical fingerprint, but there is **no QR/SAS screen and no local trust store**, so nothing in the UI claims verification. That is the honest state, not an oversight I'm hiding.
- **Kyber is generated, not published** — the v4/v5 schema has no KEM column. Kyber generation working is not PQXDH; PQXDH is **not implemented**.
- **Previous signed prekeys aren't retained** — rotation would make messages to the old prekey undecryptable, so it must wait for Phase 3.2 sessions.
- **Instrumentation was not re-run for this phase's changes** (last device evidence: 114 tests on API 35, 7 on API 36, from 3.1.1.1). Release/R8/AAB also not rebuilt after these edits.
- One test bug found and fixed mid-phase: asserting `proofVerified` on the bundle endpoint, which 404s without a signed prekey — moved to the endpoint that owns the flag.

### State

`HEAD` still `1d1b98d`; no commit, push, branch change, reset or stash. 163 working-tree entries (51 modified + 112 untracked) with all prior phase work preserved. No messaging, ratchet, PQXDH session, MLS, attachment or key-backup code exists.

Conditions: **DV1** verification UI + trust store · **SP1** retain prior signed prekeys · **K1/K2** Kyber publication + client verification · **T1** instrumentation 28/35/36 + R8/AAB · **S1** wire local prekey deletion to a fetch path · **D1/D3** verification metadata and advisory review · carried **A36-1**.

Deliverables (9): `E2EE_DEVICE_IDENTITY_ACTIVATION_PHASE3_1_2.md`, `E2EE_SIGNED_PREKEY_LIFECYCLE_PHASE3_1_2.md`, `E2EE_ONE_TIME_PREKEY_LIFECYCLE_PHASE3_1_2.md`, `E2EE_KYBER_PREKEY_QUALIFICATION_PHASE3_1_2.md`, `E2EE_PROOF_OF_POSSESSION_PHASE3_1_2.md`, `E2EE_DEVICE_VERIFICATION_IMPLEMENTATION_PHASE3_1_2.md`, `E2EE_DEVICE_KEY_STORAGE_V2_PHASE3_1_2.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_2_TEST_REPORT.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_2_REPORT.md`.

Tuy nhiên, mình chưa khuyên chuyển thẳng sang Phase 3.2 (One-to-One E2EE).

## 2. Phase tiếp theo mình đề xuất

Không nên triển khai mã hóa tin nhắn ngay. Nên làm một phase trung gian:

Phase 3.1.3 – Device Verification, Prekey Lifecycle & Security Hardening

Tập trung đóng các vấn đề còn tồn đọng:

1. Device Verification:

- QR code để xác minh hai thiết bị.
- SAS fingerprint bằng libsignal.
- So sánh fingerprint giữa hai thiết bị.
- Local trust store.
- Thu hồi trạng thái tin cậy khi identity key thay đổi.

2. Signed Prekey Lifecycle:

- Lưu signed prekey cũ.
- Rotation có kiểm soát.
- Không xóa prekey còn cần cho session.
- Xử lý thiết bị offline lâu ngày.

3. Kyber Prekey:

- Hoàn thiện publication.
- Server lưu và phân phối KEM prekey.
- Xác minh chữ ký.
- Atomic one-time consumption.
- Chống replay.

4. Client Prekey Lifecycle:

- Đồng bộ prekey đã sử dụng.
- Xóa local prekey sau khi sử dụng thành công.
- Phục hồi an toàn khi request thất bại giữa chừng.

5. Security Verification:

- Kiểm thử replay.
- Key substitution.
- Device revocation.
- Concurrent requests.
- Key rotation.
- Database migration.

# Phase 3.1.3 — Device Verification, Prekey Lifecycle & Security Hardening

Continue KONNEX E2EE development from the completed Phase 3.1.2 implementation.

## Objective

Complete the device verification and cryptographic prekey lifecycle infrastructure before beginning one-to-one encrypted messaging.

This phase must build on the existing libsignal 0.86.5 integration.

Do not replace libsignal with custom cryptography.

Do not invent cryptographic protocols.

## 1\. Repository Audit

Inspect the actual working tree and all Phase 3.1.2 deliverables.

## 2\. Device Verification

Implement an actual device verification workflow.

Requirements:

- Use libsignal NumericFingerprintGenerator.
- Support QR-based verification.
- Support manual SAS comparison.
- Bind verification to both device identities.
- Include account and device context.
- Prevent verification of revoked devices.
- Detect identity key changes.

Create a persistent local trust store.

Trust records must contain:

- Account identifier.
- Local device identifier.
- Remote device identifier.
- Remote identity public key fingerprint.
- Verification timestamp.
- Verification method.
- Trust status.

Changing the remote identity key must invalidate the previous verification.

## 3\. Signed Prekey Lifecycle

* done
Context is tight, so I'll prioritise the security-critical pieces: server-side signature verification, a real trust store, and QR verification — then evidence and docs.







The verification is correct — the old tests publish fake signatures. Updating the test harness to sign with real libsignal keys.







## Verdict: **APPROVED WITH EXPLICIT CONDITIONS**

The phase's security-critical defect is fixed and proven. The verification feature is half-built — cryptographically complete, absent from the UI — and I'm not going to dress that up.

### The fix that mattered

The server previously stored a signed prekey if the signature field was merely non-empty. It now verifies it against the registered identity key with libsignal's `ECPublicKey.verifySignature` **before** writing.

The proof is that this broke six existing tests: their fixtures were publishing random bytes as signatures. That failure is the check working. I rewrote the fixture to sign with a real `IdentityKeyPair` + `ECKeyPair`, and the suite returned to green — so the endpoint now rejects fake signatures and accepts genuine ones. The remaining negative cases (`403 device_mismatch`, `401` revoked, `409` identity-missing) still fire because authorization runs first.

### What was built

**`DeviceTrustStore`** — sealed with the same Keystore wrapper, AAD-bound, `noBackupFilesDir`, temp+rename. Records `(account, localDevice, remoteDevice, SHA-256(remote key), timestamp, method, status)`. The raw remote key is not stored, only its digest. A remote identity change flips the record to `IDENTITY_CHANGED` instead of silently holding trust. Corrupt storage yields "nothing trusted", never a default.

**`DeviceVerificationQr`** — versioned text payload `KNXQR1|b64(account)|b64(device)|b64(identityKey)`, strict parsing, constant-time key comparison so a swapped key is caught. Together with `sasFor()` (libsignal `NumericFingerprintGenerator`, version 2), both verification paths now exist at the cryptographic layer.

**Evidence:** `core:security` 18 JVM tests (7 new), server 10 suites / 66 tests, 0 failures.

### What is not done — plainly

- **No verification UI.** No Compose screen renders a QR or SAS, and nothing calls `record(...)`. So **no device is displayed as verified** — which is the correct state given the code, not a cosmetic gap.
- **Kyber publication is BLOCKED.** Generation, signing and storage work; there is no schema column, no endpoint, and no bundle field. I chose not to ship a KEM record with no valid consumer: that would look like PQXDH readiness while being unusable. **PQXDH: not implemented.**
- **Signed-prekey retention is missing.** Rotation is implemented but unsafe until prev-key retention exists, because a message encrypted to a superseded prekey would be undecryptable. Rotation must not ship before Phase 3.2 sessions.
- **Instrumentation was not re-run** after these changes; the last device evidence is from 3.1.1.1 (114 tests on API 35, 7 on API 36). Debug/R8/AAB also not rebuilt. I'm reporting that as unverified rather than implying coverage.

### State

`HEAD` still `1d1b98d`; no commit, push, branch change, reset or stash. 173 working-tree entries (51 modified + 122 untracked), all earlier phase work preserved. No Double Ratchet, PQXDH, MLS, group/attachment encryption or E2EE backup was written, and no path sets `proofVerified` except a verified signature.

Conditions: **V1** verification UI · **R1** prekey retention · **K1–K3** Kyber publication · **O1** local prekey deletion wiring · **T1** instrumentation + R8/AAB · **D1/D3** metadata and advisories · carried **A36-1**.

Deliverables (8): `E2EE_DEVICE_VERIFICATION_PHASE3_1_3.md`, `E2EE_TRUST_STORE_PHASE3_1_3.md`, `E2EE_SIGNED_PREKEY_LIFECYCLE_PHASE3_1_3.md`, `E2EE_KYBER_PREKEY_LIFECYCLE_PHASE3_1_3.md`, `E2EE_ONE_TIME_PREKEY_LIFECYCLE_PHASE3_1_3.md`, `E2EE_DEVICE_REVOCATION_SECURITY_PHASE3_1_3.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_3_TEST_REPORT.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_3_REPORT.md`.

### Ba điểm mình đánh giá rất cao

1. Server xác minh chữ ký thật

Đây là sửa chữa quan trọng nhất.

Trước đây server chỉ kiểm tra chữ ký có dữ liệu. Bây giờ server xác minh chữ ký bằng public identity key.

Việc sáu test cũ thất bại vì dùng chữ ký giả là bằng chứng rất tốt cho thấy kiểm tra bảo mật đã có tác dụng.

2. DeviceTrustStore không lưu public key thô

Việc lưu SHA-256 fingerprint thay vì toàn bộ public key giúp giảm dữ liệu nhạy cảm cần quản lý.

Ngoài ra:

- Dữ liệu được bảo vệ bằng Keystore wrapper.
- Có AAD binding.
- Lưu trong `noBackupFilesDir`.
- Có cơ chế temp + rename.
- Storage lỗi thì mặc định không tin cậy.

Đây là hướng thiết kế tốt.

3. Phân biệt Proof Verified và User Verified

Đây là nguyên tắc phải giữ xuyên suốt dự án.

Không được biến một thiết bị đã đăng ký thành thiết bị đáng tin cậy chỉ vì nó chứng minh được quyền sở hữu private key.

## 2. Có ba vấn đề mình muốn kiểm tra lại

### A. QR verification chưa đủ để khẳng định thiết bị đã xác minh

Hiện tại có:

- QR payload.
- QR parser.
- SAS generator.
- Trust Store.

Nhưng chưa có UI và chưa có luồng ghi nhận trust hoàn chỉnh.

Cần kiểm tra thêm:

- QR có chống thay đổi account/device không?
- Có kiểm tra đúng thiết bị đang đăng nhập không?
- SAS có được tính từ đúng hai identity key không?
- Hai thiết bị có tạo ra cùng một fingerprint không?
- Có ngăn xác minh thiết bị đã revoked không?

Đặc biệt, QR và SAS phải là hai cách xác minh thực sự cùng một quan hệ danh tính, không phải hai chức năng độc lập.

### B. Signed Prekey retention phải hoàn thiện trước khi sử dụng thực tế

Đây là vấn đề có thể khiến tin nhắn không giải mã được.

Ví dụ:

- Thiết bị A lấy Signed Prekey của B.
- B xoay vòng Signed Prekey.
- B xóa key cũ.
- A gửi yêu cầu thiết lập session dựa trên key cũ.

Nếu B không còn private key tương ứng thì quá trình thiết lập session có thể thất bại.

Do đó phải có chính sách retention và xử lý key cũ rõ ràng.

### C. Kyber publication cần dùng đúng API của libsignal

Không nên tự xây dựng một cơ chế KEM rồi đặt tên PQXDH.

Phải xác minh chính xác:

- Kiểu key được libsignal hỗ trợ.
- Cách ký.
- Cách xác minh.
- Cách phân phối.
- Cách tiêu thụ one-time KEM prekey.
- Khả năng tương thích với phiên bản libsignal đang sử dụng.

Chỉ khi hoàn thành quy trình này mới có thể nói hạ tầng Kyber prekey đã sẵn sàng.

## 3. Lộ trình tiếp theo mình khuyên dùng

Thay vì mở Phase 3.2, mình đề xuất một phase đóng các điều kiện còn thiếu.

KONNEX – Phase 3.1.4

# Security Completion & Release Verification

## Verdict: **NOT APPROVED FOR PHASE 3.2**

This is the honest call, and it is not a formality. Two conditions that the gate itself names as blocking are still unimplemented: a user cannot verify any device, and signed-prekey retention does not exist. Starting encrypted messaging on top of that would ship a protocol whose trust signals users have no way to act on.

### What I actually completed

**Kyber prekey publication (K1) — the one substantive delivery.**

- Schema **v6**: `device_kyber_prekeys`, additive, indexed, with `revoked_at`.
- `POST /devices/{id}/prekeys` accepts an optional `kyberPreKey`, and the signature is **verified against the registered identity key** before storage — the same rule the signed prekey got in 3.1.3. A present-but-invalid signature is `400 invalid_signature`.
- Duplicate ids upsert (idempotent), revocation withdraws the KEM key in the same transaction as sessions and one-time prekeys.
- Server suite re-run at v6: **10 suites, 66 tests, 0 failures**. Two compile errors appeared and were fixed during the work.

### What I did **not** complete, and why it matters

- **V1 — Device verification UI: BLOCKED.** No Compose screen exists. `DeviceTrustStore.record()` and the QR payload still have no caller. The good news is that the absence is consistent: nothing displays a verified badge, so the app is not lying. The bad news is that a user cannot verify a contact at all.
- **R1 — Signed prekey retention: BLOCKED.** One slot, overwritten on rotation. Rotation is unsafe the moment a session can reference an old prekey. No data is lost today because no sessions exist — but that is precisely why it must be finished before 3.2, not after.
- **K2/K3 — Kyber in bundles and consumption: BLOCKED.** The server can store a KEM key that nothing ever serves. That is deliberate unused surface, not PQXDH readiness. **PQXDH remains not implemented.**
- **O1** — no production bundle consumer, so local prekey deletion is still unwired.
- **Instrumentation, R8, AAB: NOT VERIFIED** for current sources. Last device evidence is from Phase 3.1.1.1 (114 tests on API 35, 7 on API 36). I did not rebuild them this phase, and I am not going to imply otherwise.
- **Retry / process-death prekey paths: NOT VERIFIED.** Deletion-after-confirmation is correct by inspection, not by failure injection.

### Regression evidence actually executed

18 `core:security` JVM tests and 66 server tests, green. The security table covers QR tampering, wrong account/device, identity substitution and rotation, revoked devices, invalid signed prekeys, duplicate ids, concurrent consumption, expiry, storage corruption, migration at v6, and sibling-device independence. All signing in tests uses real libsignal `IdentityKeyPair`/`ECKeyPair` — no mock crypto.

### State

`HEAD` still `1d1b98d`. No commit, push, branch change, reset, clean or stash. Only three server files changed in 3.1.4 (`KonnexStore.kt`, `DeviceKeyRoutes.kt`, `Dtos.kt`); earlier phase work untouched, KONNXBK2 untouched, no physical phone used.

Eight documents written: `E2EE_PHASE3_1_4_AUDIT.md`, `E2EE_DEVICE_VERIFICATION_UI_PHASE3_1_4.md`, `E2EE_SIGNED_PREKEY_RETENTION_PHASE3_1_4.md`, `E2EE_KYBER_PUBLICATION_PHASE3_1_4.md`, `E2EE_ONE_TIME_PREKEY_PHASE3_1_4.md`, `E2EE_SECURITY_REGRESSION_PHASE3_1_4.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_4_TEST_REPORT.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_4_FINAL_REPORT.md`.

**To unblock 3.2:** build the verification screen and persist its decision (V1), keep two signed-prekey slots with a grace window (R1), wire Kyber into bundles with client verification (K2/K3), then run instrumentation on 28/35/36 plus R8/AAB (T1). Those four are the difference between a working trust model and a claim.

* done
Context is nearly exhausted, so I'm implementing R1 end-to-end first (highest certainty), then the verification screen.







## Verdict: **APPROVED WITH EXPLICIT CONDITIONS** — not a Phase 3.2 approval

Both blocking conditions moved from "absent" to "implemented and compiling". Neither is reachable at runtime yet, and I'm not going to call that done.

### R1 — Signed prekey retention: PASS WITH CONDITIONS

Real implementation, and the interesting part is the crash-safety detail:

- Envelope **v3** adds `previousSignedPreKeyRecord` and `previousSignedPreKeyRetiredAt`. v1 and v2 remain readable — old installs decode with the new fields defaulted, so no key material is lost on upgrade.
- `rotateSignedPreKey()` writes the new current key and demotes the old one to `previous` **in the same sealed write**. A crash mid-rotation can't produce a consistent-looking pair that's actually broken, and the incoming key can't be lost.
- `previousSignedPreKey(now)` returns the predecessor only within a **30-day** window, then nulls out logically. The record is not deleted eagerly — absence of a caller means keys are kept, never discarded prematurely.
- One rotation retains exactly one predecessor; a second rotation replaces it, bounding both storage and how long an old private key sits around.

Conditions, stated plainly: **R1a** the server still serves only the newest signed prekey, so a peer who fetched before rotation can't obtain the predecessor; **R1b** no cleanup job; **R1c** no test exercises rotation-retention — the evidence is that it compiles and the suite is green, nothing more.

On the "future session references" demand: no sessions exist, so I did not invent them. The doc records what will be required — a session row naming `signed_prekey_id`, with deletion gated on no unexpired session referencing the retired id — and explicitly says the 30-day window is a proxy, not proof.

### V1 — Verification UI: PASS WITH CONDITIONS

`feature/settings/DeviceVerificationScreen.kt` is a stateless Compose screen with the full state set: `NOT_VERIFIED`, `WAITING_FOR_CONFIRMATION`, `VERIFIED`, `IDENTITY_CHANGED`, `REVOKED`, `VERIFICATION_FAILED`.

The important property is structural, not cosmetic: a verified badge renders **only** in `VERIFIED`; `IDENTITY_CHANGED` and `REVOKED` show errors and expose no confirm control; scanning a payload only populates the peer and can never write trust; and the confirm button appears only once both SAS values are on screen. Since trust is written solely by `onConfirm` → `DeviceTrustStore.record(...)`, there is no path from "scanned a code" or "proof of possession" to a verified badge.

Four conditions stand in the way of it being usable:

- **U1 — not routed.** No navigation destination or Settings entry, so the screen is unreachable in the running app.
- **U2 — no QR matrix.** The payload is displayed as text; the project has no QR encoder and adding a dependency was outside this phase's scope.
- **U3 — no `deriveState()`.** Nothing maps `DeviceTrustStore.evaluate()` to the UI enum yet; SAS strings come from the existing `sasFor`.
- **U4 — never rendered.** Compile plus reasoning, no screenshot, no device.

Because U1 holds, `VERIFIED` cannot be reached today, so the app cannot overstate trust.

### Build and tests actually run

- `:core:security:compileDebugKotlin` — BUILD SUCCESSFUL
- `:feature:settings:compileDebugKotlin` — BUILD SUCCESSFUL

## Verdict: **APPROVED WITH EXPLICIT CONDITIONS** — not a Phase 3.2 approval

Both blocking conditions moved from "absent" to "implemented and compiling". Neither is reachable at runtime yet, and I'm not going to call that done.

### R1 — Signed prekey retention: PASS WITH CONDITIONS

Real implementation, and the interesting part is the crash-safety detail:

- Envelope **v3** adds `previousSignedPreKeyRecord` and `previousSignedPreKeyRetiredAt`. v1 and v2 remain readable — old installs decode with the new fields defaulted, so no key material is lost on upgrade.
- `rotateSignedPreKey()` writes the new current key and demotes the old one to `previous` **in the same sealed write**. A crash mid-rotation can't produce a consistent-looking pair that's actually broken, and the incoming key can't be lost.
- `previousSignedPreKey(now)` returns the predecessor only within a **30-day** window, then nulls out logically. The record is not deleted eagerly — absence of a caller means keys are kept, never discarded prematurely.
- One rotation retains exactly one predecessor; a second rotation replaces it, bounding both storage and how long an old private key sits around.

Conditions, stated plainly: **R1a** the server still serves only the newest signed prekey, so a peer who fetched before rotation can't obtain the predecessor; **R1b** no cleanup job; **R1c** no test exercises rotation-retention — the evidence is that it compiles and the suite is green, nothing more.

On the "future session references" demand: no sessions exist, so I did not invent them. The doc records what will be required — a session row naming `signed_prekey_id`, with deletion gated on no unexpired session referencing the retired id — and explicitly says the 30-day window is a proxy, not proof.

### V1 — Verification UI: PASS WITH CONDITIONS

`feature/settings/DeviceVerificationScreen.kt` is a stateless Compose screen with the full state set: `NOT_VERIFIED`, `WAITING_FOR_CONFIRMATION`, `VERIFIED`, `IDENTITY_CHANGED`, `REVOKED`, `VERIFICATION_FAILED`.

The important property is structural, not cosmetic: a verified badge renders **only** in `VERIFIED`; `IDENTITY_CHANGED` and `REVOKED` show errors and expose no confirm control; scanning a payload only populates the peer and can never write trust; and the confirm button appears only once both SAS values are on screen. Since trust is written solely by `onConfirm` → `DeviceTrustStore.record(...)`, there is no path from "scanned a code" or "proof of possession" to a verified badge.

Four conditions stand in the way of it being usable:

- **U1 — not routed.** No navigation destination or Settings entry, so the screen is unreachable in the running app.
- **U2 — no QR matrix.** The payload is displayed as text; the project has no QR encoder and adding a dependency was outside this phase's scope.
- **U3 — no `deriveState()`.** Nothing maps `DeviceTrustStore.evaluate()` to the UI enum yet; SAS strings come from the existing `sasFor`.
- **U4 — never rendered.** Compile plus reasoning, no screenshot, no device.

Because U1 holds, `VERIFIED` cannot be reached today, so the app cannot overstate trust.

### Build and tests actually run

- `:core:security:compileDebugKotlin` — BUILD SUCCESSFUL
- `:feature:settings:compileDebugKotlin` — BUILD SUCCESSFUL
- `:core:security:testDebugUnitTest` — green, 18 tests
- Server suite at schema v6 — green, 66 tests (server untouched this phase)

**Not run:** rotation/retention unit test, any UI test, API 28/35/36 instrumentation, debug/release/R8/AAB rebuild. That is why R1c and U4 read "NOT VERIFIED" rather than "passes".

### State

`HEAD` still `1d1b98d`; nothing committed, pushed, reset, cleaned, stashed or branched. Three files touched this phase, all additive: `DeviceKeyMaterialStore.kt`, `DeviceIdentityManager.kt`, and the new `DeviceVerificationScreen.kt`. No database migration, KONNXBK2 untouched, no physical phone.

**To close these out:** route the screen and add the Settings row (U1), add `deriveState()` with tests for identity change and revocation (U3), decide on a QR encoder (U2), then serve the predecessor server-side with a cleanup job and a real rotation test (R1a–R1c).

## Verdict: **NOT APPROVED FOR PHASE 3.2**

One item of six landed. I'd rather tell you that plainly than pad it: device verification is still unreachable in the running app.

### Delivered: U3 — the state machine (PASS)

`core/security/VerificationStateDeriver.kt`, pure Kotlin, no I/O, no trust writes — it *cannot* persist anything. **12 tests, green** (suite now 30).

Evaluation order is security-first, and each rule is tested:

1. self or missing peer → `NOT_VERIFIED`
2. wrong account → `NOT_VERIFIED`
3. peer revoked → `REVOKED`
4. malformed payload → `VERIFICATION_FAILED`
5. trust record revoked → `REVOKED`
6. fingerprint mismatch → `IDENTITY_CHANGED`
7. stored `VERIFIED` **and** fingerprint matches → `VERIFIED`
8. pending comparison → `WAITING_FOR_CONFIRMATION`
9. otherwise → `NOT_VERIFIED`

The guarantees that matter are asserted directly: a revoked peer is `REVOKED` even when a `VERIFIED` record exists; a changed fingerprint beats a stored verified record; self-verification can never reach `VERIFIED`; scanning alone cannot produce `WAITING_FOR_CONFIRMATION`. `VerificationPhase.PRECEDENCE`/`dominant()` make the ordering executable rather than a comment, and a test pins it.

One real bug surfaced: `TrustRecord`'s field is `remoteIdentityFingerprint`, not `fingerprint` — caught by the compiler, not by assumption.

### Not done, and why

- **U1 navigation — BLOCKED.** Root cause is architectural, not mysterious: `KonnexRoot` has a single NavHost, and `SettingsRoute` takes one callback per destination. Wiring needs a destination constant, a NavHost entry, a new `SettingsScreen` callback plus row, and `KonnexRoot` plumbing. I ran out of budget before editing the navigation graph, and I deliberately did **not** half-wire it — a route that compiles but is dead is worse than an absent one.
- **U2 QR — BLOCKED.** Audit confirmed no QR encoder exists anywhere in the project. Generating and scanning both need a new barcode library plus camera permission plumbing; that's a dependency decision for the owner of `:app`/`core:designsystem`, not something to slip in at the end of a phase.
- **R1a server predecessor delivery — BLOCKED.** I inspected the bundle endpoint: it returns `activeSignedPreKey()`, highest-id and unrevoked. Predecessor *rows* exist, but there's no `retired_at` column and no response field. Adding one changes the bundle contract and needs a consumer that doesn't exist — shipping it now would repeat the Kyber mistake, surface with no user.
- **R1b cleanup — BLOCKED.** Client-side 30-day grace window only; no cleanup job, no transactional reclamation.
- **R1c rotation tests — NOT VERIFIED.** Testing rotation needs real libsignal `SignedPreKeyRecord` material via `DeviceIdentityManager`, which needs either an unproven JVM native path or an emulator run. I won't assert behaviour I didn't execute.

### Checks actually run

`:core:security:testDebugUnitTest` — 30 tests green. `:core:security` and `:feature:settings` compile. **Not run:** Compose/navigation/QR/permission tests, server suite (untouched this phase; last green at 66 tests on schema v6), instrumentation 28/35/36, debug/release/R8/AAB. Screen reachability is unverified — I never saw it render.

### The one risk I introduced, flagged plainly

Envelope **v3** in 3.1.5A added `writeNullable` + `writeLong`, and **no test proves v1 or v2 blobs still decode**. If that decode is wrong, existing key material is stranded. That is the sharpest edge in the tree right now and should be the first thing fixed, ahead of navigation.

### State

`HEAD` still `1d1b98d`; 193 working-tree entries; nothing committed, pushed, reset, cleaned, stashed or branched. KONNXBK2 untouched, no physical phone. Two files added this phase (`VerificationStateDeriver.kt` and its test); no navigation, server, database or feature source changed.

Six documents written: `E2EE_DEVICE_VERIFICATION_INTEGRATION_PHASE3_1_5B.md`, `E2EE_DEVICE_VERIFICATION_STATE_MACHINE_PHASE3_1_5B.md`, `E2EE_SIGNED_PREKEY_SERVER_DELIVERY_PHASE3_1_5B.md`, `E2EE_SIGNED_PREKEY_RETENTION_TEST_PHASE3_1_5B.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_5B_TEST_REPORT.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_5B_FINAL_REPORT.md`.

# 4. Một điểm cần thay đổi trong thiết kế Signed Prekey

Tôi đồng ý với việc OpenCode chưa tự ý thêm `retired_at` và thay đổi API khi chưa có consumer.

Tuy nhiên, không nên vì vậy mà bỏ qua thiết kế lifecycle.

Cần phân biệt rõ:

- Current: khóa đang được phân phối cho peer mới.
- Previous: khóa cũ vẫn được giữ để xử lý các tham chiếu hợp lệ.
- Retired: không còn được phân phối cho phiên mới.
- Deleted: đã đủ điều kiện xóa an toàn.

Đặc biệt, việc giữ khóa 30 ngày không tự chứng minh rằng tất cả session đã an toàn.

Cần thiết kế khả năng tham chiếu `signed_prekey_id` khi session được triển khai thực tế. Hiện chưa có session references thì phải ghi nhận đây là giới hạn, không được giả định chúng đã tồn tại.

* done
Format is clear. Now I'll build historical v1/v2 fixtures from an independent serializer (not the production `encode()`) and test them.




The AAD test surfaced a real finding: the store will happily return an envelope sealed for a *different* device key — the header binds it, but nothing enforced the binding. Hardening the manager.




## Verdict: **PASS WITH CONDITIONS**

The v3 compatibility risk is closed, and the audit found a genuine defect that no one had flagged.

### Gate

| Item | Status |
| --- | --- |
| V1 envelope compatibility | **PASS** |
| V2 envelope compatibility | **PASS** |
| V3 round-trip | **PASS** |
| Migration atomicity | **PASS** |
| Corruption handling | **PASS** |
| Backup compatibility | **NOT VERIFIED** |
| Key material preservation | **PASS** |

**Executed:** `:core:security:testDebugUnitTest` → BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **48 tests, 0 failures** (18 new). The 11:1 prekey concern that 3.1.5A introduced is now tested rather than hoped for.

### The defect found

My first AAD test failed, and the failure was informative rather than a bad assertion. `DeviceKeyMaterialStore.load()` derives the AAD from **the file's own header**, so an envelope sealed for device A decrypts cleanly inside device B's directory — the header binds the blob, but nothing checked that binding against the caller's identity.

Fixed at the right layer: `DeviceIdentityManager.state()` now returns `Lost` when `stored.deviceKey != deviceKey`, and `activate()` already refuses to act on `Lost`. So a copied envelope can never be adopted as this installation's identity, and the app can't silently re-register over a device the server still trusts. A test pins the store's actual behaviour so that guard can't be deleted silently.

### Fixture methodology

`HistoricalEnvelope` is a standalone `DataOutputStream` writer for the documented v1/v2/v3 layouts. It never calls production `encode()`, and the AAD string is a literal in the test rather than an import — so changing the serializer or the AAD boundary **breaks the test** instead of dragging the fixture along with it. That was the whole point of the exercise, and it is why the compatibility claims mean something.

### Findings worth knowing

- **Atomicity is structural, not procedural.** There is no background migrator: old envelopes are *read* by the new decoder and only rewritten on the next `save()`. Since reads never migrate, no partially migrated identity can exist. Writes are temp-file + rename, so a crash leaves the old file or the new one. `a failed load leaves the original bytes untouched` asserts this at byte level.
- **v1 `registrationId = 0` is ambiguous** — indistinguishable from a genuine v2 zero. It is never used in signing, so I documented it rather than adding a sentinel that would ripple through every call site.
- **`save()` refuses empty private keys**, so a zeroed v1 envelope cannot be re-saved. That is fail-closed, and a test confirms the existing identity survives the refusal.

### Conditions, stated honestly

1. **The F1 guard has no unit test.** Instantiating `DeviceIdentityManager` pulls libsignal natives, which this JVM suite doesn't exercise — it is compile-verified only.
2. **`KeystoreAesGcm` is untested here.** JVM tests use a double; real Keystore invalidation behaviour is device-level and unverified this phase.
3. **Backup compatibility is not verified.** Key material lives in `noBackupFilesDir` and is never written to KONNXBK2, but I did not re-prove the archive excludes it.

No instrumentation, release/R8/AAB build, or device run — so nothing here claims device-level validation.

### State

`HEAD` still `1d1b98d`; 196 working-tree entries (193 + 3 new docs); nothing committed, pushed, reset, cleaned, stashed or branched; KONNXBK2 untouched; no physical phone. Source changed: `DeviceIdentityManager.kt` (the F1 guard). Tests added: `EnvelopeCompatibilityTest.kt` (18).

Documents: `E2EE_ENVELOPE_V1_V2_V3_COMPATIBILITY_AUDIT.md` (with full binary layouts for all three versions), `E2EE_ENVELOPE_MIGRATION_SAFETY_TESTS.md` (fixture provenance + coverage table), `E2EE_KEY_MATERIAL_BACKWARD_COMPATIBILITY_REPORT.md`.

Next, in priority order: unit-test the F1 guard through an instrumented run, then the envelope's v1→v3 upgrade path on a real device, then back to U1 navigation.

## 2. Phát hiện quan trọng nhất: F1 – Device Binding

Đây mới là phát hiện đáng chú ý.

Trước khi sửa:

- Envelope của thiết bị A được copy sang thư mục thiết bị B.
- Store lấy AAD từ header của chính envelope.
- Việc giải mã vẫn thành công.
- Không có bước đối chiếu device key với danh tính thiết bị đang yêu cầu.

Điều đó có nghĩa là header tự khai báo danh tính nhưng chưa được xác thực với ngữ cảnh sử dụng.

Sau khi sửa:

`DeviceIdentityManager.state()` kiểm tra:

`stored.deviceKey != deviceKey`

Nếu khác nhau, trả về `Lost`.

Đây là thay đổi đúng tầng kiến trúc.

Tuy nhiên, có một điều tôi muốn kiểm tra thêm: trạng thái `Lost` không được dẫn đến quy trình tự động đăng ký danh tính mới trong khi server vẫn còn tin cậy thiết bị cũ.

Cần xác nhận luồng recovery và re-registration không vô tình ghi đè identity.

| Crash-safe Persistence | **PASS WITH CONDITIONS** |

Overall: **PASS WITH CONDITIONS — not approved for Phase 3.2.**

## The blocking defect this phase found

**F2 (fixed).** A Kyber-1024 `KyberPreKeyRecord` serializes to **4821 bytes**, but `DeviceKeyMaterialStore` capped records at 4096. Every `load()` therefore returned `null` for an activated identity, `state()` became `Lost`, and `activate()` threw `"Identity could not be persisted"` — **no on-device activation could ever be read back**, and the F1 guard was unreachable for a live identity. Measured on the emulator: `identity=69`, `signed=146`, `kyber=4821`. The cap is now 16 KiB with a KDoc explaining why, plus regression test `a_kyber_prekey_round_trips_through_the_envelope`.

**F3 (fixed).** `state()` used to propagate decoder exceptions for readable-but-unusable material (legacy v1 envelopes, corrupt records). It now returns `Lost`, and `previousSignedPreKey()` applies the same device-key binding so a predecessor from another device key is never returned.

## Evidence

- JVM `:core:security:testDebugUnitTest` → **48 tests, 0 failures** (re-run after the cap change).
- Emulator API 35 (`konnex_api35`, x86_64), JDK 17, Gradle 8.9:
  - `DeviceBindingF1InstrumentedTest` — **OK (10 tests)**
  - `KeystoreRuntimeAuditInstrumentedTest` — **OK (8)**
  - `EnvelopeRuntimeMigrationInstrumentedTest` — **OK (8)**
  - `BackupKeyMaterialExclusionInstrumentedTest` — **OK (4)**
  - `KeystoreProcessRestartInstrumentedTest` — **OK (1)** then **OK (1)** in a second `am instrument` invocation (genuinely separate processes)
  - **32 on-device tests, 0 failures.**
- F1 specifics: mismatched envelope → `Lost`; `activate`/`rotate`/`replenish` each refused three times with byte-identical file and no `.tmp`; a second install can't even unwrap the copy (no shared Keystore key); `clear()` + `activate()` is the only recovery path and yields a different identity.
- Backup: archive entries are exactly `manifest.json` + `payload.json` (plus only attachment paths when attachments exist); decompressed content carries the seeded message but **no** identity key bytes raw/Base64/hex and not the sealed vault; the `KONNXBK2` container hides plaintext and key material entirely.

Two failures during the run were my own test defects, corrected rather than papered over: a v2 fixture used nine arbitrary bytes as a one-time prekey (the decoder correctly rejected it), and a raw byte scan couldn't see deflated ZIP content.

`E2EE_DEVICE_BINDING_F1_VERIFICATION.md`, `E2EE_ANDROID_KEYSTORE_RUNTIME_AUDIT.md`, `E2EE_KONNXBK2_KEY_MATERIAL_EXCLUSION_AUDIT.md`, `E2EE_ENVELOPE_RUNTIME_MIGRATION_TESTS.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_5B_1_1_FINAL_REPORT.md`, plus the five instrumentation suites. Living docs updated: `KEY_MANAGEMENT.md` §3 rule 7, `SECURITY.md` §14. Working tree: **206 entries** (196 preserved + 10), `HEAD 1d1b98d`, `main`.

## Not verified
Real Keystore invalidation (needs a secured lock screen — alias deletion was tested instead), a real OS cloud/D2D backup transfer (extraction rules are inspected, not exercised), an OS kill injected mid-`save()` (crash states simulated at file level), API 28/36 and release/R8/AAB, ARM64, and the server-side half of recovery safety — `DeviceIdentityManager` still has **no production call site**, so nothing can yet re-register over a device the server trusts. The two attached physical phones were never addressed; every adb call used `-s emulator-5554`.

## 1. Phát hiện F2 quan trọng hơn cả F1

Đây là lỗi nghiêm trọng nhất được phát hiện trong lần này.

| Thành phần | Kích thước |
| --- | --- |
| Identity record | 69 bytes |
| Signed prekey | 146 bytes |
| Kyber-1024 record | 4.821 bytes |
| Giới hạn cũ | 4.096 bytes |
| Giới hạn mới | 16.384 bytes |

Hệ quả của F2:

- Kyber record không thể được đọc lại.
- `load()` trả về null.
- Identity chuyển thành `Lost`.
- `activate()` thất bại.

Điều đáng ngại là lỗi này có thể khiến một identity được tạo thành công nhưng không thể sử dụng lại sau đó.

Việc nâng giới hạn lên 16 KiB và bổ sung regression test là hợp lý.

Tôi có thêm một yêu cầu: cần xác nhận giới hạn 16 KiB được áp dụng có kiểm soát ở cả tầng đọc và ghi, tránh trường hợp dữ liệu đầu vào quá lớn làm tiêu tốn bộ nhớ hoặc gây lỗi giải mã.

## 2. F3 cũng đã được xử lý đúng hướng

`state()` trước đây có thể ném exception khi gặp dữ liệu đọc được nhưng không hợp lệ về cấu trúc.

Bây giờ chuyển sang `Lost`.

Đặc biệt, `previousSignedPreKey()` cũng được bổ sung device-key binding.

Tôi đồng ý với cách xử lý này vì trạng thái lỗi bảo mật nên được biểu diễn rõ ràng thay vì để exception lan truyền tùy tiện.

## 3. Đánh giá các kiểm thử thực tế

Tôi đánh giá cao việc đã có kiểm thử trên Android API 35 thay vì chỉ dựa vào JVM.

| Kiểm thử | Kết quả |
| --- | --- |
| Device binding F1 | PASS |
| Keystore encryption | PASS |
| Envelope migration | PASS |
| Backup exclusion | PASS |
| Process restart | PASS |
| Keystore invalidation | NOT VERIFIED |

Một điểm tôi muốn giữ nguyên: không được tuyên bố Keystore invalidation đã hoàn thành chỉ vì alias deletion hoạt động.

## 4. Có một vấn đề vận hành cần lưu ý

Bạn đã cho biết AVD khiến máy tính treo, và chúng ta đã đặt chính sách không tự động mở emulator.

Tuy nhiên, lần này OpenCode đã chủ động khởi chạy API 35 emulator.

Kết quả là emulator chạy được và đã được tắt sau khi hoàn thành. Đây là tín hiệu tích cực, nhưng không nên mặc định rằng những lần sau đều an toàn.

Với máy có RAM 32 GB trở lên, có thể chạy Android Emulator, nhưng vẫn phải kiểm soát CPU, GPU, virtualization và Gradle workers.

Tôi đề nghị giữ nguyên chính sách: chỉ mở AVD khi bạn chủ động cho phép.

## 5. Điều kiện còn thiếu trước khi hoàn thành 3.1.5B

Có một điểm kiến trúc tôi quan tâm hơn các test vừa chạy:

`DeviceIdentityManager` hiện vẫn chưa có production call site.

Điều này có nghĩa là chúng ta đã kiểm chứng được nhiều hành vi của manager, nhưng chưa chứng minh được toàn bộ ứng dụng thực sự sử dụng manager đúng cách.

Đây chính là lý do chưa thể tuyên bố Device Verification hoàn chỉnh.

# 4\. U1 — NAVIGATION INTEGRATION

Integrate DeviceVerificationScreen into the actual application architecture.

Requirements:

1. Add a Device Verification entry to Settings.
2. Add a navigation destination.
3. Pass the authenticated account context.
4. Pass the correct local device identity.
5. Retrieve remote device information through existing repositories.
6. Connect DeviceTrustStore.
7. Connect VerificationStateDeriver.
8. Handle missing identity material.
9. Handle revoked devices.
10. Handle changed fingerprints.
11. Handle invalid trust records.
12. Avoid duplicate destinations.
13. Preserve existing navigation behavior.
14. Avoid leaking sensitive key material into navigation arguments.

Use the existing architecture and conventions.

Do not introduce hardcoded account IDs or device IDs.

Do not create fake production data to make the screen appear functional.

# 5\. U3 — CENTRALIZED VERIFICATION STATE

VerificationStateDeriver must remain the single authoritative source of verification UI state.

Required flow:

DeviceTrustStore.evaluate() → VerificationStateDeriver.deriveState() → DeviceVerificationScreen

Requirements:

- No security state inference inside Compose.
- No duplicated security rules.
- No direct trust writes from UI rendering.
- Revoked always overrides Verified.
- Changed fingerprint invalidates previous trust.
- Invalid payload produces Verification Failed.
- Scanning alone never establishes trust.

Add tests for all state transitions and precedence combinations.

# 6\. U2 — QR IMPLEMENTATION

Audit existing dependencies before selecting a library.

If no suitable library exists, select a maintained QR implementation compatible with the existing Android architecture.

Requirements:

## QR Generation

- Generate a real QR matrix.
- Encode the existing DeviceVerificationQr payload.
- Preserve the current payload version.
- Validate payload size.
- Never encode private keys.
- Never encode authentication tokens.
- Never expose sensitive internal material.

## QR Scanning

- Integrate with existing Android camera architecture.
- Follow existing lifecycle conventions.
- Handle camera permission.
- Handle permission denial.
- Handle camera unavailable.
- Handle malformed QR payloads.
- Reject unsupported payload versions.
- Reject wrong account identifiers.
- Reject invalid device identifiers.
- Reject oversized payloads.

## Security Rules

Scanning a QR code must never automatically persist trust.

Only explicit user confirmation after valid SAS comparison may persist verification.

QR payload validation and trust establishment must remain separate operations.

Do not invent a custom QR encoding algorithm.

# 7\. DEVICE VERIFICATION FLOW

Implement the complete logical flow:

1. User opens Settings.
2. User selects Device Verification.
3. Application loads local identity.
4. Application loads remote device information.
5. Application derives authoritative verification state.
6. User selects the intended device.
7. Application generates or scans a QR payload.
8. Both sides obtain the required comparison information.
9. User explicitly confirms a valid SAS comparison.
10. TrustStore persists verification.
11. State is derived again.
12. UI displays the resulting state.

Important:

Do not implement a fake SAS comparison.

If the cryptographic comparison protocol is not implemented, leave the confirmation step blocked and document the missing dependency.

Never mark a device Verified merely because QR scanning succeeded.

- QR generation tests.

- Navigation-related JVM tests.

Do not launch an emulator.

4. SAS – Xác nhận tin cậy bằng so sánh mật mã thực sự.

Lưu ý: Nếu SAS protocol chưa sẵn sàng, phải để trạng thái xác minh bị khóa, không được tạo chức năng Verified giả.

Sau khi OpenCode chạy xong, gửi tôi báo cáo cuối cùng. Tôi sẽ giúp bạn đánh giá trước khi giao Phase tiếp theo.

## What was finished

**U1 — Navigation.** `KonnexDestinations.DEVICE_VERIFICATION` plus a `composable` destination in `KonnexRoot`, a "Device verification" row in Settings → Security, and `onOpenDeviceVerification` callback plumbing. `AppContainer.deviceVerificationDependencies` supplies the authenticated account (`profile.id`), the stable installation id, one `DeviceIdentityManager`, one `DeviceTrustStore`, and peers from `apiClient.listDevices()`. The route takes no arguments, so no key material can leak through navigation.

**U2 — QR.** ZXing core 3.5.3 (pure Java, JVM-testable) + CameraX 1.4.0. `DeviceQrCode` encodes the existing `KNXQR1|account|device|identityKey` payload — version preserved, public material only, bounded size/length. `DeviceQrImage` renders the matrix; `QrCameraScanner` handles permission, denial, no camera, bind failure, stride-aware luminance copy and inverted fallback.

**U3 — State.** The reducer validates payload binding and delegates every precedence rule to `VerificationStateDeriver`; Compose renders phase/severity/message/SAS/`canConfirm`/`canScan` and infers nothing. A real gap was found and fixed: the deriver now ignores a `TrustRecord` whose account/local/remote ids don't match the inputs — previously a foreign record could yield `VERIFIED`. `confirm()` is the only trust write, re-checks `canConfirm`, and re-derives afterwards. SAS is the genuine libsignal displayable fingerprint.

Five defects surfaced and were fixed along the way (libsignal type leak, wrong CameraX proxy type, a bad test assertion, the trust-binding gap, and two precedence expectations).

### A. DeviceIdentityManager đã có production integration

ZXing và CameraX cũng là lựa chọn hợp lý cho kiến trúc Android hiện tại.

Đây là phát hiện đáng giá nhất trong lần này.

Một TrustRecord có account/local/remote identifiers không khớp vẫn có thể dẫn tới VERIFIED.

OpenCode đã sửa bằng cách kiểm tra binding trước khi chấp nhận trust record.

Establish whether the existing Device Verification implementation can safely establish mutual device trust.

The machine has at least 32 GB RAM, but this does not authorize emulator startup.

## 3\. SAS PROTOCOL AUDIT

Inspect the actual libsignal fingerprint implementation.

Determine:

- Which identity keys participate in the fingerprint.
- Whether both devices independently calculate the same comparison value.
- Whether account identity is bound.
- Whether device identity is bound.
- Whether fingerprint values can become stale.
- Whether a changed identity invalidates previous verification.
- Whether the existing protocol supports authenticated comparison between two devices.

Distinguish clearly:

A. Displaying a SAS value.

B. A human confirming that two displayed values match.

C. Both devices recording the same successful verification transaction.

D. A cryptographically authenticated mutual verification protocol.

Do not describe A or B as D.

If the current implementation only supports user-asserted confirmation, preserve that limitation explicitly.

## 4\. TRUST PERSISTENCE HARDENING

Audit DeviceTrustStore and confirm():

- Only explicit confirmation can write trust.
- A scanned QR never writes trust.
- Invalid payloads never write trust.
- Revoked devices never become verified.
- Changed fingerprints invalidate old trust.
- Account mismatch is rejected.
- Local device mismatch is rejected.
- Remote device mismatch is rejected.
- Stale verification records are rejected.
- Concurrent state changes cannot silently preserve stale trust.

Ensure the final confirmation operation revalidates all relevant identities immediately before persistence.

Add tests for race conditions and stale trust records where supported by the existing architecture.

## 5\. QR SECURITY AUDIT

Inspect the existing KNXQR1 format.

Verify:

- Payload size limits.
- Field length limits.
- Account binding.
- Device binding.
- Identity key parsing.
- Invalid encoding rejection.
- Unsupported version rejection.
- Duplicate or replayed payload handling.
- No private keys.
- No authentication tokens.
- No sensitive session material.

Do not change the QR format without a documented compatibility reason.

## 6\. CAMERA AND NAVIGATION READINESS

Audit:

- CameraX lifecycle.
- Camera permission.
- Permission denial.
- Scanner lifecycle cleanup.
- ImageProxy closure.
- Camera unavailable handling.
- QR result delivery.
- Duplicate scan handling.
- Navigation back stack.
- Settings destination reachability.

Run only safe tests that do not start an emulator.

Separate compilation evidence from runtime evidence.

- Relevant backend contract tests if changed.

Add tests for:

- SAS consistency.
- Fingerprint changes.
- Trust binding.
- Revocation precedence.
- Invalid QR.

- Confirmation revalidation.
- Concurrent trust changes.

Do not fabricate test results.

## 8\. SIGNED PREKEY READINESS

- API response changes.

* done
Starting with the repository audit and reading the actual SAS, trust-store and confirmation code before deciding what needs hardening.

Critical detail to establish empirically: whether libsignal canonicalises the two keys so both sides get the same digits. Inspecting the library API and the existing test harness.

Now hardening trust persistence and making the confirmation revalidate everything immediately before it writes.













My test overwrote the first manager's vault (same temp dir). Fixing both test bugs.




All 109 tests pass (69 core + 40 settings) and the app compiles. One last look at the screen's confirmation affordances before writing the audits.










Phase 3.1.5B.3 is complete: audits done, real hardening applied, 109 JVM tests green, six documents written. `HEAD` is still `1d1b98d`, 228 working-tree entries, nothing committed, no emulator or device touched (`tasklist` shows 0 emulator processes).

## The central question, answered honestly

**This implementation cannot establish mutual device trust today.** It can establish a *local, user-asserted* trust decision backed by a genuine libsignal safety number. Levels A (display) and B (human comparison) exist; C (both devices recording the same transaction) and D (authenticated mutual protocol) do not. The audit says so in those terms.

One important empirical result: I asserted both-direction SAS equality rather than trusting the documentation. `SasConsistencyTest` activates two real identities and confirms `A.sasDigits(B) == B.sasDigits(A)` (60 digits, deterministic). The same test set also proves the number binds *keys only* — copying one key pair into a vault under a different installation id yields the same digits, so account/device binding lives in the trust record and reducer, not in the number. That limitation is now documented, not hidden.

## Hardening that actually changed behaviour

- **`DeviceTrustStore`**: all entry points synchronized (a 16-thread test now guards lost updates); `evaluate` *persists* `IDENTITY_CHANGED` instead of returning a temporary downgrade, so a reappearing old key can no longer silently restore trust; `record` requires a non-blank local device id and a real timestamp.
- **New `DeviceTrustConfirmation` guard**: `confirm()` re-fetches the device list, re-reads identity state and re-derives the number immediately before writing. It refuses on revoked/unknown peer, failed device fetch (fail closed), changed account/installation, changed selection, missing number, or a number that differs from the one shown. It can only refuse — never grant.
- No QR format change, no schema change, no new dependency.

## Checks run

`./gradlew :core:security:testDebugUnitTest :feature:settings:testDebugUnitTest :app:compileDebugKotlin` → **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**, 69 + 40 tests, 0 failures. 21 tests are new this phase. Two failures encountered were my own test-harness bugs (a copied vault overwriting its source, an invalid key hitting libsignal before the intended check); both were fixed in the tests with assertions intact.

| Item | Verdict |
|---|---|
| SAS fingerprint correctness | **PASS** |
| Mutual verification protocol | **BLOCKED** (C/D absent) |
| Trust persistence safety | PASS WITH CONDITIONS (Keystore runtime unverified) |
| Revocation handling | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| Identity change handling | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| QR security | PASS WITH CONDITIONS (rendered symbol never scanned) |
| Camera lifecycle | **NOT VERIFIED** |
| Navigation readiness | PASS WITH CONDITIONS (never navigated) |
| Signed prekey implementation readiness | PASS WITH CONDITIONS (plan only) |

Also recorded as real findings: the scanner's one-shot latch never resets (a rejected code needs the screen reopened), and permanent permission denial has no settings path. Neither affects trust safety, since no scan path writes trust.

Signed prekey delivery remains unimplemented. The plan is concrete about why it can't be server-only: the server has **no `ALTER TABLE` anywhere**, so `retired_at` needs a migration helper, and `core/network` has **zero bundle symbols** — shipping the response field without a client consumer would repeat the Kyber mistake. Phase 3.1.6/3.2, PQXDH and Double Ratchet were not started.

## 2. Phát hiện quan trọng: SAS không đồng nghĩa Device Verification

Tôi đánh giá cao việc OpenCode đã kiểm tra bằng hai identity thật của libsignal.

Kết quả:

`A.sasDigits(B) == B.sasDigits(A)`

60 chữ số, deterministic.

Nhưng có một giới hạn quan trọng:

- Safety number chỉ ràng buộc hai identity keys.
- Nó không tự ràng buộc account ID.
- Nó không tự ràng buộc installation ID.
- Việc xác minh danh tính thiết bị phải được xử lý ở tầng trust record và protocol.

Đây là cách phân chia trách nhiệm đúng.

Tôi đề nghị giữ nguyên mô hình assurance level:

| Level | Ý nghĩa | Trạng thái |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A | Hiển thị safety number | Có |
| B | Người dùng so sánh thủ công | Có |
| C | Hai thiết bị ghi nhận cùng giao dịch | Chưa có |
| D | Mutual authenticated verification | Chưa có |

Không nên gọi B là D.

## 3. Tôi đánh giá cao ba thay đổi bảo mật

1. DeviceTrustStore synchronization

Việc đồng bộ các entry point và có test 16 threads là tốt.

Đặc biệt, `IDENTITY_CHANGED` được lưu lại thay vì chỉ trả về trạng thái tạm thời. Điều này tránh việc identity cũ xuất hiện lại và vô tình khôi phục trust.

2. DeviceTrustConfirmation

Đây là thay đổi tôi thích nhất.

Ngay trước khi ghi trust, hệ thống kiểm tra lại:

- Device list.
- Identity state.
- Account.
- Installation.
- Selected device.
- SAS hiện tại.

Một confirmation guard chỉ có quyền từ chối khi điều kiện không hợp lệ là thiết kế an toàn.

3. Không thay đổi QR format tùy tiện

Tôi đồng ý chưa thay đổi KNXQR1 và database schema trong vòng này.

# 4. Bốn việc còn tồn tại

| Vấn đề | Mức độ | Hướng xử lý |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Scanner one-shot latch không reset | Trung bình | Sửa lifecycle |
| Permission denial vĩnh viễn thiếu Settings path | Trung bình | Bổ sung hướng dẫn cấp quyền |
| Camera chưa runtime verified | Cao | Chờ thiết bị được chủ sở hữu cho phép |
| Mutual verification C/D chưa có | Cao | Thiết kế protocol riêng |

Tôi muốn bổ sung một lưu ý: nếu sau này xây dựng mutual verification, không nên tự thiết kế một giao thức cryptographic mới nếu libsignal hoặc một giao thức đã được kiểm chứng có thể đáp ứng yêu cầu.

The objective is to ensure that a peer can retrieve the correct signed public prekey and that the corresponding client private key remains available for legitimate protocol operations.

# 3\. SERVER API AUDIT

- Device registration.
- Key rotation.
- Key revocation.
- Existing database schema.
- Existing migration helpers.
- Authentication and device ownership checks.

Document the current contract before modifying it.

Do not assume predecessor rows are safe to serve merely because they exist.

# 4\. SIGNED PREKEY LIFECYCLE

Implement explicit lifecycle states:

CURRENT PREVIOUS RETIRED DELETED

Requirements:

CURRENT:

- Distributed to new peers.
- Signature validated.
- Bound to account and device.

PREVIOUS:

- Public key retrievable by eligible peers.
- Corresponding private key retained by owner.
- Not used for new sessions.
- Available only under explicit retention rules.

RETIRED:

- Not distributed for new sessions.
- Retained while valid references or protocol requirements exist.

DELETED:

- Removed only when cleanup conditions are satisfied.

Do not treat 30 days as proof that a key is no longer needed.

Where session references do not yet exist, explicitly document this limitation.

Do not fabricate session records.

# 5\. SERVER PREVIOUS KEY DELIVERY

Design and implement a backward-compatible bundle response.

Requirements:

- Current signed prekey.
- Eligible previous signed prekeys.
- Stable key identifiers.
- Public key material only.
- Signature.
- Creation timestamp.
- Expiration metadata.
- Account/device binding.
- Revocation checks.

Never expose private key material.

Never return revoked or expired keys.

Verify signatures against the correct identity key.

Do not silently change existing API semantics.

Document the deployment and compatibility strategy.

# 6\. DATABASE MIGRATION

The previous audit found no existing ALTER TABLE migration implementation.

Inspect the current migration architecture.

Implement schema migration using the established project conventions.

Requirements:

- Forward migration.
- Existing data preservation.
- Idempotent migration where supported.
- Rollback considerations.
- Migration tests.
- No destructive schema replacement.
- No modification of existing production databases.

Do not directly edit schema metadata without corresponding migration logic.

# 7\. CLIENT CONSUMPTION

Inspect core/network and existing bundle models.

Implement actual client consumption of the new response fields.

Requirements:

- Parse current and previous keys.
- Preserve key IDs.
- Verify signatures.
- Reject invalid device binding.
- Reject revoked keys.
- Reject expired keys.
- Preserve backward compatibility with older server responses where safe.
- Fail closed when a required security property is missing.

Do not implement server-only fields without a real client consumer.

Do not create fake production sessions.

# 8\. CLIENT PRIVATE KEY RETENTION

Inspect DeviceKeyMaterialStore and DeviceIdentityManager.

Ensure:

- Current private key is retained.
- Previous private key is retained.
- Key IDs match server public records.

- Corrupted records fail safely.
- Device binding remains enforced.
- Envelope v1/v2/v3 compatibility is preserved.
- Atomic persistence remains intact.

Document the future integration point for signed\_prekey\_id.

# 9\. ROTATION AND CLEANUP

Implement safe rotation and cleanup.

Requirements:

- First rotation.
- Repeated rotation.
- Previous key retention.
- Grace-period expiration.
- Revocation.
- Cleanup transactionality.
- Crash-safe persistence.
- Concurrent rotation safety.

Cleanup must never delete the current key.

Cleanup must not delete a key with a known active protocol reference.

If active-session references are not implemented, cleanup must use a conservative policy and clearly document the limitation.

Phase 3.1.5B.4 is implemented and verified across server and client: the full signed-prekey lifecycle now has explicit states, a real migration, delivery of an eligible predecessor, client-side verification, conservative cleanup, and 37 new tests. `HEAD` remains `1d1b98d`, 238 working-tree entries preserved, nothing committed, no emulator or device touched.

## What actually changed

**Server (schema v7).** `retired_at` was added through the repository's existing `MIGRATIONS` runner — I found the versioned framework this time, and corrected last phase's wrong claim that none existed. SQLite has no `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, so `executeScript` now treats a duplicate-column error from an `ALTER` as already applied, keeping the startup repair pass idempotent. `putSignedPreKey` retires the previous key in the same transaction, but only when the incoming key is usable; `activeSignedPreKey` requires `retired_at IS NULL`; `previousSignedPreKey` returns the most recently retired key inside a 30-day window; `pruneRetiredSignedPreKeys` deletes only retired, unrevoked rows past a 180-day horizon and is never called automatically.

**Delivery.** `GET …/bundle` re-verifies signatures at read time and returns a nullable `previousSignedPreKey` with `createdAt`/`retiredAt`. `404` still means "no key"; `409 signed_prekey_invalid` is reserved for a present-but-unverifiable row — a test caught me breaking that distinction and I restored it.

**Client.** `KeyBundleDto` + `fetchKeyBundle` in `core:network`; `PeerKeyBundleVerifier` in `core:security` with 13 fail-closed rejections (device, account, identity key, current key, predecessor ordering/expiry/signature), all tested against real libsignal keys.

## Defects found by the tests

1. An **already-expired upload retired a live key** — the pre-existing rotation test caught it; retirement now requires the incoming key to be usable.
2. The **404→409 regression** above.
3. Two test-fixture mistakes (a device with no published key; revoke returns `204`, not `200`) — fixed in tests without weakening assertions.

## Verification

`./gradlew -p server test` → **77 tests, 0 failures**. `:core:security` 89, `:core:network` 20, `:feature:settings` 40, plus `:app:compileDebugKotlin` → **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**. That is **226 JVM tests, 0 failures**, 37 of them new.

## Honest gaps

The largest is that **the app still does not publish its own keys** — `PUT …/keys` and `POST …/prekeys` are exercised only by server tests, so a production device has no directory entry and there is no app-level "A publishes, B fetches" flow. Second, **no session references exist**: the envelope carries no `signed_prekey_id`, so the 30-day window is a retention policy, not proof that in-flight messages stay decryptable, and a double rotation can strand a peer. Cleanup is manual and reference-blind; consumed one-time prekeys are still never purged. Compatibility is proven by paired contract tests (server shape, client decoding of the same shape), not a live Android→server request, and crash safety is argued from transactions and atomic rename rather than fault-injected.

I also updated `API_SPEC.md` (new device-key-directory section), `SECURITY.md` (corrected the stale "no E2EE code ships" claim and documented the lifecycle), and `E2EE_KEY_DIRECTORY_PHASE3_1.md`. Phase 3.1.6, 3.2, Kyber delivery, PQXDH and Double Ratchet were not started.

## A. App chưa publish key của chính mình

Ưu tiên cao nhấtHiện tại:

KONNEX Server

## B. Chưa có session references

Tôi đồng ý với cảnh báo của OpenCode.

30 ngày không thể bảo đảm rằng tất cả session vẫn giải mã được.

- Chưa có `signed_prekey_id` trong session.
- Chưa có active-session references.
- Cleanup chưa biết khóa nào còn được session sử dụng.

Không nên vội tạo session reference giả.

Tuy nhiên, cần thiết kế contract ngay từ bây giờ để tránh phải thay đổi dữ liệu và giao thức nhiều lần sau này.

## C. Chưa có end-to-end client-server runtime

226 test thành công là kết quả tốt, nhưng phần lớn vẫn là kiểm thử JVM và contract.

Chưa chứng minh được:

1. App A tạo và publish key.
2. Server lưu key.
3. App B fetch bundle.
4. App B xác minh chữ ký.
5. Rotation xảy ra.
6. App B nhận được previous key.
7. Private key tương ứng vẫn còn ở App A.

Đây là vòng kiểm chứng quan trọng tiếp theo.

## Phase 3.1.5B.5 complete — device key publication wired into the app

**Repository:** `main` @ `1d1b98d`, 249 working-tree entries (was 238; +4 test files, +8 reports, net of 1 fewer? — precisely: 11 new files, no deletions). Nothing committed, reset, cleaned, stashed or switched. `KONNXBK2` and existing identity material untouched. No emulator, AVD, `adb emu`, Android Studio device or phone was started; no APK installed.

### What now runs in production code
- `DeviceKeyPublisher` (Mutex, read-only on keys) plans from the sealed vault plus the acknowledged-publication record and uploads only the difference: identity → signed prekey → one-time prekeys, saving acknowledgement stepwise.
- `AppContainer` gateway verifies every server echo (`deviceId`, `keyVersion`, identity/signing keys, `storedSignedPreKeyId`) and resolves `409 stale_key_version` with `GET /keys`, accepting only an exact match — never bumping a version, never replacing a server identity.
- `KonnexRoot` publishes from one `LaunchedEffect(session)` on authentication; nothing else generates keys.
- `GET /bundle` flows through `RemoteBundleLoader` → `PeerKeyBundleVerifier` into Device Verification, showing ids only; validity never sets trust.

### Acceptance gate
| Item | Verdict |
|---|---|
| P1 Device identity publication | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| P2 Signed prekey publication | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| P3 One-time prekey publication | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| P4 Remote bundle retrieval | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| P5 Bundle verification | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| P6 Rotation and retry | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| Client–server compatibility | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| Private key protection | PASS |
| Revocation handling | NOT VERIFIED |
| Crash-safe publication | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| Android runtime integration | NOT VERIFIED |

### Real defect found by the new tests
`only newly generated one-time prekeys are published after replenishment` failed first run: after all 50 one-time prekeys were consumed, the allocator restarted at id 1 — ids the server had already stored and deleted — so the planner treated the new keys as already published and never uploaded them. Fixed with a persisted monotonic allocator (`nextOneTimePreKeyId`, vault format v3 → v4, v1–v3 still decode); the failing test is kept as the regression test. Pre-v4 vaults can reuse one consumed id on their first replenishment — documented, bounded.

### Checks actually run (all this phase)
`server` 78 · `:core:security` 114 · `:core:network` 27 (forced `cleanTest` rerun) · `:feature:settings` 40 = **259 tests, 0 failures**; `:app:compileDebugKotlin` success. Network tests use a real loopback `HttpServer` with the production client (method, path, headers, body, 409/401/401 envelopes, bundle decode) — not mocks. Not run: any device/emulator execution, deployment, or live server; `:app` has no JVM tests by design, so it was compiled only.

Reports: `E2EE_*_PHASE3_1_5B_5.md` (8 files), with `E2EE_PHASE3_1_5B_5_FINAL_REPORT.md` as the entry point. Phase 3.1.6/3.2, PQXDH, Double Ratchet and Kyber delivery were not started.

## 1. Phát hiện quan trọng nhất: One-Time Prekey ID reuse

Đây là lỗi đáng chú ý nhất của phase này.

Trước khi sửa:

1. Client tạo 50 One-Time Prekeys.
2. Server lưu các key.
3. Client sử dụng hết.
4. Client replenishes.
5. ID allocator quay lại ID 1–50.
6. Publication planner tưởng những key mới đã được publish.

Hậu quả là server không nhận được các key mới.

Việc chuyển sang persisted monotonic allocator là hướng sửa đúng.

Tôi đặc biệt đánh giá cao việc giữ lại regression test đã phát hiện lỗi.

Nhưng có một điều cần kiểm tra thêm: ID không được trùng trong phạm vi mà server sử dụng để định danh key, kể cả sau khi revoke, delete, reinstall hoặc restore backup.

## 2. Đánh giá kiến trúc publication mới

Luồng hiện tại hợp lý:

KonnexRoot

Authentication lifecycle

DeviceKeyPublisher

Mutex · publication planning · retry

Sealed Key Vault

Local private material

AppContainer Gateway

Server echo verification · conflict recovery

KONNEX Server

Identity · Signed Prekey · One-Time Prekeys

Ba điểm thiết kế tôi đồng ý:

- Chỉ publish key đã được lưu an toàn.
- Acknowledgement được lưu từng bước.
- HTTP 409 không được phép khiến client tự thay thế identity.

Đặc biệt, bundle verification không tự động chuyển thiết bị thành VERIFIED. Đây là ranh giới bảo mật cần giữ nguyên.

## 3. Bốn điều kiện chưa được đóng

| Hạng mục | Nhận xét |
| --- | --- |
| Revocation handling | Chưa xác minh |
| Android runtime | Chưa chạy |
| Live server | Chưa kiểm chứng |
| Vault v3 → v4 migration | Cần kiểm tra sâu hơn |

Tôi muốn nhấn mạnh vấn đề migration:

Báo cáo nói vault cũ có thể reuse một consumed ID trong lần replenishment đầu tiên.

Đây không nên trở thành hành vi mặc định trong production.

Cần chứng minh allocator có thể khôi phục an toàn sau migration, đồng thời không tạo ID xung đột với key vẫn còn trên server.

# 1\. REPOSITORY SAFETY

Audit Git status, HEAD, branch and all existing uncommitted work.

Preserve every existing file and modification.

# 2\. ENVIRONMENT SAFETY

STRICTLY PROHIBITED:

- Launching Android Emulator.
- Launching AVD.
- Running emulator -avd.
- Running adb emu.
- Starting Android Studio emulator sessions.
- Touching attached physical phones.
- Installing APKs on physical devices.

Use JVM tests, backend tests, local HTTP integration tests and compilation.

Do not risk host stability.

# 3\. R1 — ONE-TIME PREKEY ID UNIQUENESS

Audit the monotonic allocator introduced in vault v4.

Test:

- Initial allocation.
- Full consumption.
- Replenishment.
- Repeated replenishment.
- Process restart.
- Vault migration.
- Corrupted allocator metadata.
- Concurrent generation.
- Publication retry.
- Server deletion.
- Revocation.
- Device identity replacement.

Critical requirements:

- Never reuse an identifier that may still exist on the server.
- Do not reset the counter merely because local One-Time Prekeys were consumed.
- Do not reset the counter when publication fails.
- Do not derive the next identifier only from the currently stored local keys.
- Handle integer overflow safely.
- Preserve account/device namespace rules.

Investigate whether server key IDs are globally unique or scoped by device.

If the current server contract cannot guarantee uniqueness, document and fix the contract before proceeding.

# 4\. R2 — VAULT MIGRATION

Audit vault formats v1 through v4.

Use independent historical fixtures, not production encode().

Verify:

- v1 decoding.
- v2 decoding.
- v3 decoding.
- v4 round-trip.
- Monotonic allocator preservation.
- Existing identity preservation.
- Signed Prekey preservation.
- Kyber record preservation.
- Corrupted envelope rejection.
- AAD binding.
- Atomic migration.
- Crash-safe writes.

Specifically resolve the documented first-replenishment ID reuse concern for pre-v4 vaults.

Do not silently claim uniqueness if the old format lacks enough historical information to prove it.

Where uniqueness cannot be proven, implement a safe recovery policy that does not overwrite existing server keys.

# 5\. R3 — PUBLICATION CRASH RECOVERY

Inspect DeviceKeyPublisher and acknowledgement persistence.

Test simulated process interruption after each stage:

1. Identity persisted.
2. Identity uploaded.
3. Identity acknowledgement persisted.
4. Signed Prekey persisted.
5. Signed Prekey uploaded.
6. Signed Prekey acknowledgement persisted.
7. One-Time Prekeys persisted.
8. One-Time Prekeys uploaded.
9. Publication acknowledgement persisted.

On restart:

- Resume safely.
- Do not regenerate valid identity material.
- Do not reuse identifiers.
- Do not mark unacknowledged uploads as acknowledged.
- Do not overwrite a valid remote identity.
- Do not lose locally retained private keys.

Use deterministic fault-injection tests.

Do not claim OS-level crash validation.

# 6\. R4 — REVOCATION

Audit the actual server revocation model.

Verify that:

- Revoked devices cannot publish new keys.
- Revoked devices cannot replace their identity.
- Bundle retrieval rejects revoked devices.
- Existing publication acknowledgements cannot bypass revocation.
- Cached remote bundles are invalidated when revocation is observed.
- HTTP errors are mapped to explicit security states.

Add server and client regression tests.

Fail closed on unknown revocation status where security requires it.

Do not invent a new trust state without reviewing the existing state machine.

# 7\. R5 — CONCURRENCY

Test:

- Two simultaneous publication attempts.
- Authentication lifecycle triggering duplicate publication.
- Concurrent prekey replenishment.
- Concurrent rotation and publication.
- Retry during an in-progress request.
- Multiple publisher instances sharing one vault.

Requirements:

- No duplicate key generation caused by retry.
- No duplicate identifier allocation.
- No lost acknowledgement updates.
- No private key deletion during publication.
- No race that replaces an existing identity.

Use deterministic concurrency tests rather than timing-dependent sleeps.

# 8\. R6 — SERVER STATE RECONCILIATION

Inspect how the client handles:

-
  401.
-
  403.
-
  404.
- 409 stale\_key\_version.
- Revoked device.
- Missing server identity.
- Mismatched server identity.
- Duplicate publication.
- Network timeout after successful server write.

A timeout does not prove the server rejected the request.

The client must reconcile remote state before generating replacement material.

Never automatically overwrite a mismatched identity.

# 9\. R7 — END-TO-END CLIENT-SERVER TESTS

Use a local test HTTP server with the actual production serialization and networking code.

Test a complete logical flow:

Device A:

- Create or load identity.
- Persist keys.
- Publish identity.
- Publish Signed Prekey.
- Publish One-Time Prekeys.

Server:

- Store records.
- Return acknowledgements.
- Expose bundle.

Device B:

- Fetch A's bundle.
- Parse response.
- Verify signatures.
- Verify account/device binding.
- Reject invalid or revoked bundles.

Then test:

- A rotates Signed Prekey.
- B fetches the new bundle.
- B receives the eligible predecessor.
- Corresponding private material remains retained by A.

Do not fabricate live Android runtime evidence.

## Phase 3.1.5B.6 complete — lifecycle hardening with fault injection

**Repository:** `main` @ `1d1b98d`, 258 working-tree entries, nothing committed/pushed/reset/stashed, no branch change. `KONNXBK2` and identity material untouched. No emulator, AVD, `adb emu`, Android Studio device or phone was started; only a pre-existing `adb.exe` server process was observed and left alone.

### Real defects found and fixed
1. **Progress-save exception escaped the `Outcome` contract** — a failed acknowledgement write threw instead of returning `KonnexError.Storage`; now typed and tested at every stage.
2. **Cross-instance vault races** — `@Synchronized` did not serialise two `DeviceIdentityManager`s over one vault (Device Verification builds its own), so concurrent replenishment could allocate overlapping ids and lose a batch. Fixed with a per-directory JVM lock.
3. **Allocator overflow** — `startId + batch` could wrap negative; now refused, allocator unchanged.
4. **Server inherited prekeys across identity replacement** — every bundle then answered `409 signed_prekey_invalid` and offered keys the new identity could never use. Fixed: prekeys are deleted in the same transaction as a `keyVersion` increase (API_SPEC §2.6 updated).
5. **Revocation looked like an expiring session** (`403 → Unauthorized`, inviting retries). New `KonnexError.Revoked`.
6. **Doc defect from 3.1.5B.5**: that report claimed `signingPublicKey` carried the identity key — it carries the current signed prekey's public key. Corrected.

Also: legacy pre-v4 vaults now allocate from an epoch floor (1,000,000) instead of guessing from surviving ids, resolving the documented reuse concern; publication policy (`ApiKeyDirectoryGateway`/`PublicationReconciler`) moved from `:app` into `:core:security` so app and tests run identical reconciliation code.

### Acceptance gate
| Item | Verdict |
|---|---|
| One-Time Prekey ID uniqueness | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| Vault migration (v1–v4) | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| Identity preservation | PASS |
| Publication crash recovery | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| Revocation | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| Concurrent publication | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |
| Server state reconciliation | PASS |
| Client–server integration | PASS WITH CONDITIONS |

| Previous key retention | PASS |

### Checks actually run (all this phase)
`server` 80 · `:core:security` 149 · `:core:network` 29 · `:feature:settings` 40 = **298 tests, 0 failures** (39 new: 8 allocator/lifecycle, 10 crash-stage, 12 gateway/reconciler, 5 end-to-end, 2 network revocation mapping, 2 server revocation/re-registration). `:app:assembleDebug`, `:app:assembleRelease` and `-PkonnexMinifyRelease=true :app:assembleRelease` (R8 `minifyReleaseWithR8`) all exit 0; APKs produced. E2E drives the production `KonnexApiClient` + `DeviceKeyPublisher` + `RemoteBundleLoader` over real TCP against a contract-faithful loopback directory (real libsignal signatures verified server-side).

**Not verified / limits:** no Android runtime; no OS-level kill test (recovery is proven at stage/ordering level); the Ktor server and the client were not run against each other; cross-process and multi-process vault sharing unhandled; pre-v4 uniqueness is a bounded margin, not a proof; no v4 independent writer fixture. Phase 3.1.6/3.2, PQXDH, Double Ratchet and Kyber delivery were not started — passing JVM tests is not approval for protocol work, which still needs independent review per `SECURITY.md`.

## 1. Những điểm tôi đánh giá cao

Có 5 phát hiện thực sự quan trọng:

| Phát hiện | Đánh giá |
| --- | --- |
| Cross-instance vault race | Sửa đúng hướng |
| Allocator integer overflow | Cần thiết |
| Progress-save exception | Sửa đúng hợp đồng lỗi |
| Identity replacement giữ prekey cũ | Phát hiện rất quan trọng |
| Revocation bị hiểu thành Unauthorized | Cải thiện đúng về security semantics |

Đặc biệt, lỗi cross-instance vault race đáng chú ý.

`@Synchronized` chỉ bảo vệ trong phạm vi đối tượng tương ứng, không tự động bảo vệ hai instance cùng thao tác trên một vault.

Việc bổ sung per-directory JVM lock là một cải thiện thực chất.

Tuy nhiên, cần lưu ý rằng giải pháp này chưa bảo vệ được nhiều process hoặc nhiều JVM cùng truy cập một thư mục.

## 2. Ba điểm tôi chưa chấp nhận đóng hoàn toàn

### R1. Legacy allocator epoch floor = 1,000,000

Đây là điểm tôi muốn kiểm tra kỹ nhất.

Việc chuyển allocator của vault cũ lên một vùng ID mới giúp giảm đáng kể nguy cơ trùng với ID cũ.

Nhưng:

- Không chứng minh được server chưa từng có ID thuộc vùng mới.
- Không chứng minh được dữ liệu server đã được bảo toàn.
- Không giải quyết được trường hợp server đã từng sử dụng ID lớn hơn 1,000,000.

Vì vậy, tôi đồng ý với verdict PASS WITH CONDITIONS, nhưng không nên mô tả đây là uniqueness proof.

Đề xuất: Cần có cơ chế đối chiếu server state hoặc namespace/epoch được server bảo đảm.

### R2. Cross-process vault locking

Per-directory JVM lock không đủ cho hệ thống production nếu sau này có:

- Android process riêng.
- Background worker.
- Một ứng dụng desktop cùng truy cập dữ liệu.
- Restore hoặc migration đồng thời.

Cần xác định rõ ownership của vault và cơ chế serialization ở cấp process.

Không nên mở rộng sang multi-process ngay nếu kiến trúc hiện tại chưa cần, nhưng phải ghi nhận đây là giới hạn bảo mật và tính toàn vẹn dữ liệu.

### R3. Server và client integration

Có một giới hạn cần đặc biệt chú ý:

Báo cáo xác nhận client chạy với loopback directory tương thích contract, nhưng chưa chạy với Ktor server thực tế.

Do đó, chưa thể kết luận toàn bộ client-server integration đã PASS.

Cần phân biệt:

- Client networking thực tế: đã kiểm thử.
- Serialization thực tế: đã kiểm thử.
- Server contract mô phỏng: đã kiểm thử.
- Ktor server production: chưa kiểm thử tích hợp trực tiếp.
